The unique great devotee, Nambigal, stood there, The Lord cast His divine gaze upon him. The simile of the calf and the cow is frequently used in many places, and the way the author employs it here is particularly excellent. We have seen the convention where Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is likened to a calf and the Lord to its mother, who nourishes and protects it, as in “like a calf that hears the lowing of its mother” and “like a new mother cow that cries out when its calf is restrained and called.” At the same time, in the Thiru Puranam of Thiru Kannappa Deva Nayanar, we saw that the divine Sekkizhar Peruman likened the Lord to a calf and Kannappa Deva Nayanar to its protective mother in the phrase “like a calf separated from its new mother.” The Peruman, praised as “the daughter of the mountain king, the left half of the body,”

Here, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman states, “Nerizhai onrum valappaaka meniyar,” referring to the abundance of the Mother’s presence. Although both “the Mother is the right half of the Father” and “the Father is the left half of the Mother” convey the same meaning, here, the Lord’s divine grace is the male form, and compassion is the form of the Mother. Due to the overflowing abundance of that divine grace, it is specifically stated as “Nerizhai onrum valappaaka meniyar.” Such a Peruman, Lord Shiva, looking at Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, said, “Aroorane, you have come to save this world.” He graciously called and accepted him, saying, “Aroorane, you have come to save this world.” As it is said, “The South, which performed great penance, lived,” meaning, “You have come to save this world.” “You have come to save this world by singing the faultless Thiru-thondar Thogai.”

Thus the Peruman bestows His grace. “The Lord’s own divine intention is to ask, ‘Why have you come?’” This is a sacred saying from Thevaram.

Thus, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman praises the greatness of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, that unique great devotee. The forty-third Thiru-padal: “Adiyen pizhai poruththu ennai aandu kondu aththodakkinai neekki mudivilaa neritharum perungarunai en tharaththatho ena munnar padiyoon nenjodu panmurai paninthezhum paramparai aananda vadivu ninrathu ponru inba vellaththu malarnthanar van thondar peruman.” Sundaramoorthy Swamigal supplicates to the Lord, who graciously said, “Aroorane, you have come.” “O Lord, forgive the fault committed by this servant, accept me as Your own, remove the attachment caused by the fault, and bestow upon me the endless supreme path of Your sacred feet.” He praises, “Is Your great compassion, O Lord, worthy of my humble state?” “The fault I committed is great, but Your compassion in forgiving that great fault and accepting me is even greater. I am utterly unworthy of receiving such great compassion.” It is the nature of great souls to thus acknowledge their own insignificance and unworthiness, and to praise the greatness of the Lord’s compassion.

Here, we recall the Thiru-thandakam of Thiru Navukkarasu Peruman: “Piththanen pedaiyen peyen nayen pizhaiththanaigal aththanaiyum emparamoyo emperuman thirukkarunai irunthavare.” The fault that Nambigal mentions here refers to the incident that occurred earlier in Thiru Kailai, and the lawsuit where he said, “I am not a slave,” which we contemplated in the Purana of his being taken into service. He refers to it thus, praising and with a melting heart, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal bows and rises many times. He bowed and rose many times, exceeding the prescribed number of times, due to his overwhelming emotion. Like a continuous flow of water, one after another, without interruption, as if bliss itself had taken a form, Van Thondar Peruman Sundaramoorthy Swamigal was immersed in a flood of joy. It was as if bliss itself had taken a form. The experiencer, the object of experience, and the bliss itself—this is the Triputi consciousness.

To stand as pure love itself, that is what is meant by ‘as if bliss had taken a form.’ Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is immersed and reveling in the flood of Shivanantha. The forty-fourth Thiru-padal, The standing Vanthondar, with water-adorned matted locks, approached the Lord’s lotus feet. He went and announced that Cheralan, the king, was outside the jeweled gate. The Lord, who wields the mountain as a bow, commanded the great Deva (Nandi) to go and bring him. He arrived and, having received the victorious celestials’ grace, quickly came to worship His feet.

Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, the great Vanthondar, who was as if bliss itself had taken a form, approached the Lord, whose matted locks bear the Ganga, and supplicated, “Cheralan, Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, is outside the jeweled gate, the Thiru Anukkan Thiru-vaayil, of Your temple.” This is Nambigal’s supplication to the Lord on behalf of his friend Cheraman. These are the good associations that the Lord Himself brought about and reminded him of. We see that they yield good results appropriate to their respective situations. We are shown that the association with great people and good conduct yield such excellent results. When thus supplicated, the Peruman, who has Meru as His bow, commanded Thiru Nandi Devar, who is a great Deva, revered as Apara Shiva, to go and bring him. As soon as Thiru Nandi Devar went and announced that the Lord’s command had been received for the victorious Cheraman Perumal Nayanar to enter, Cheraman Perumal Nayanar quickly came to worship the Lord’s feet.

The forty-fifth Thiru-padal: “Mangai paagartham thirumunbu seythaaga vanthiththu magizhvaithi pongum anbinil serallaru potrida pu”

The Lord of Kailai, whose long matted locks bear the Ganga where the new moon wanders, showed the ray of His divine smile and graciously asked, “Why have you come here uninvited?” Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, with increasing love, praised Lord Shiva, whose sacred form has Uma as His right half, standing at a distance before Him, worshiping and rejoicing.

The Lord of Kailai, whose long matted locks bear the Ganga where the crescent moon wanders, made a divine smile towards Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, showing a sweet light, and graciously asked, “Why have you come here uninvited?” The ray of the Lord’s divine smile indicates His acknowledgment of Cheraman Perumal Nayanar’s arrival. The Lord’s smile burned down the three cities.

Here, similarly, The Lord’s divine smile became a smile of grace. It was a ‘kumizh sirippu’ (a blossoming smile) praised by Appar Peruman.

The Lord graciously asked Cheraman Perumal, “Why have you come here uninvited?” The forty-sixth Thiru-padal: “Arasar anjali kooppi ninru adiyen aroorar kazhal potri purasai yanai mun seviththu vanthanan pozhiyum nin karunai thendirai seytren viraisey konrai seru veniyaa ini oru vinnappam ullathu enru enbathu thiruppadal.” The Lord graciously asked Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, “Why have you come here uninvited?” The king, standing with folded hands, Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, with hands folded above his head, replied, “This servant, Nambiyaaru, I came worshiping his sacred feet, serving him in front of the elephant with the frontal plate that he rode upon, by his grace.” This is the first part of Cheranar’s reply to the Lord’s gracious question, asked with a smile, “Why have you come here uninvited?” “I, this servant, did not intend to come to Thiru Kailai. I cannot be separated from Nambi Aroorar, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal.

“Therefore, with the intention of serving Nambi Aroorar without separation, I came serving him in front of Nambi Aroorar Peruman’s elephant. That service brought me to the Thiru Anukkan Thiru-vaayil of Your Lordship.” Having said this first, Cheraman Perumal Nayanar now states the second part: “The grace that brought me, who was standing at the Thiru Anukkan Thiru-vaayil, to Your presence here, That grace is not my doing either. It is the great flood of Your grace, Your divine command, that propelled me and brought me into Your presence.

“Therefore, in both these ways, I came not with the intention of coming here, but as an act of divine grace,” says Cheraman Perumal Nayanar. Cheraman Perumal Nayanar’s words demonstrate great strength and the profound devotion of one who has lost self-consciousness. These are to be seen and followed for the world to be uplifted. It was the great flood-like divine grace that brought him from the Thiru Anukkan Thiru-vaayil to the Lord’s presence. He says he was caught in it and entered.

“Thus, there was no independent action; it was all by the Lord’s grace. Cheraman Perumal Nayanar praises and supplicates, mentioning the act of Nandi Peruman calling him by the Lord’s command. “Konrai ser veniyaa,” He addresses the Peruman as “Konrai ser veniyaa.” The Konrai flower is a special emblem of Lord Shiva, His sacred garland.

“Furthermore, Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, having worshiped from Thiru Anjaikkalam, which he regularly worshiped, entered. The Sthala Vriksham (sacred tree) of Thiru Anjaikkalam is the Konrai tree.

“Therefore, addressing the Lord of Thiru Kailai as “Konrai ser veniyaa,” Cheraman Perumal Nayanar supplicates, “There is yet another request.” Let us go on contemplating this.”
Thiruchitrambalam.


Chapter 47 — Vellanai Charukkam 11

Thiruchitrambalam.
The Thiru-Peruman of Thiru-Seikkizhar, in the Thiru-Periya Puranam, Part 1033 and the concluding part, Cheraman Perumal Nayanar submits to the Lord that he has one more request. The forty-seventh Thiru-Padal: “Perugu Vedamum Munivarum Thuthipparum Perumaiya Unai Anbal Thiru-Ula-Puram Padinen Thiru-Sevi Saththidap Peravendum Maruvu Pasaththai Akandida Vanthondar Kootam Vaiththai Enna Arulum Eesarum Solluga Endranar Anbarum Ketpiththar.” Cheraman Perumal Nayanar submits: “O Lord Shiva, whose greatness is known to the ever-expanding Vedas and the sages who praise You! Out of love, I have sung the Thiru-Ula-Purappattu for You. Please listen to it with Your sacred ears.” He submits, “Please listen, O Lord, who has gathered the company of the strong devotees to free them from the bonds of attachment.

“Your greatness is beyond praise. If even the great Vedas, Your own words, and the great sages who have realized You in their hearts cannot fully praise You, who else can? Even knowing this, I, Your humble servant, have sung the Thiru-Ula-Puram to praise You, not out of a sense of my own ability to praise, but out of the power of love.” This Thiru-Ula-Puram is a type of Tamil literary work, a ‘Ula’ within the ‘Purappattu’ genre, which falls under the ‘Purappuram’ section of the twelve ‘Purathinai’ (external poetic themes), specifically under ‘Padan Thinai’. It is one of the forty-seven sub-genres, categorized under ‘Kadavul Mattu Manida Pendir Nayan’. This ‘Ula’ work was the first ‘Ula’ ever sung in Tamil, hence it is also known as ‘Aadhi Ula’. It was composed on the Lord of Kailash with the core idea of Shiva-gnanam (divine knowledge) through ‘Pathi Bodham’ (divine teaching) and ‘Shiva Bodham’ (divine realization).

“Therefore, this ‘Prabandham’ (literary work) is also revered as ‘Thiru-Kailaya Gnana Ula’ and, being divine, as ‘Deiva Ula’. This ‘Prabandham’ was compiled by Nambiandar Nambigal Peruman in the Eleventh Thiru-Murai. He submits that this ‘Prabandham’ should be heard. “You have removed the ‘Anava Malam’ (egoistic impurity) and the ‘Malam’ (other impurities), and you have removed the ‘Maruvu Pasam’ (binding attachment).” That is, through the worship of the Lord, one attains the removal of ‘Anava Malam’ and the state of receiving Shiva’s grace.

“Furthermore, “You have gathered the company of the strong devotees,” referring to the divine act of uniting Sundaramurthy Swamigal with them. The state of being freed from ‘Vasana Malam’ (subtle impurities) through association with the company of devotees, all these are explained in the ‘Gnana Sastram’ (scriptures of wisdom), as the true meaning of the twelfth sutra of ‘Shiva Gnana Bodham’. Cheraman Perumal Nayanar thus submitted, and the Lord, who is all grace, said, “Speak.” The Lord, whose actions are all grace, and whose fivefold functions are all acts of grace, as stated in the ‘Gnana Sastram’, was present in the heart of Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, inspiring him to sing. The Lord, who was there to bestow grace by listening, thus gave His consent. Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, having sung out of love, presented it as if he were speaking to the Lord, and making Him listen, even though the Lord, who can hear everything without anyone speaking, already knew. This was due to the abundance of his love.

“Thus, he made Him listen. The eighth Thiru-Padal: The Lord, who is half-female, graciously accepted the Thiru-Ula-Puram presented by the Chera King with devotion, and with great pleasure, He said, “You two, along with Aalala Sundaran, who is the native of Oor, shall reside here as the leaders of our Ganas.” The Lord graciously accepted the Thiru-Ula-Purappattu presented by Cheraman Perumal Nayanar with love, and with a pleased heart, the Lord, who is half-female, with great joy and grace, declared: “You two, along with Nambi Aruran, who is Aalala Sundaran, shall reside here as the leaders of our Ganas.” Here, the Lord graciously declared, “You two, along with Aalala Sundaran, who was formerly Aalala Sundaran and went to the South as Nambi Aruran according to Our command, and has now returned here, shall reside here as the leaders of our Ganas.”

“The Lord graciously declared, “You two shall reside here as the leaders of our Ganas, having attained a state from which you will not return.” Thus, He graciously declared that they both should reside there without separation. The forty-ninth Thiru-Padal: In that manner, both bowed and rose, accepting the Lord’s grace upon their heads. The strong devotee Aalala Sundaran, having become Aalala Sundaran again, continued his former good deeds. The chief Chera Peruman also undertook the delightful service that they performed. As the Lord thus bestowed His grace, in that manner, both great souls bowed and rose, accepting the Lord’s grace upon their heads. They continued to live according to the Lord’s command.

“Nambigal Sundaramurthy Swamigal continued the sacred service he had performed in his former state as Aalala Sundaran. These were primarily acts such as offering flower garlands to the Lord and carrying sacred ash. Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, having become a benevolent Shiva Gana Natha, also undertook the beloved sacred service that they performed. This describes Cheraman Perumal Nayanar’s state in Kailash. The fiftieth Thiru-Padal: “Thalathu Vanthu Mun Uthayam Sei Paravaiyar Sangiliyar Ennum Nalathin Mikavar Valvinai Thodakkara Nayaki Arulale Alaththa Melladik Kamaliniaarudan Aninthithaiyar Aaki Malaiththanip Peruman Magal Koyilil Tham Thozhil Vazhi Nindrar.” Both Paravaiyar and Sangiliyar, who were born on this earth and possessed great virtues, returned to Thiru-Kailash. Through the grace of Umaiyammaiyar, they both attained their former state, having been freed from the strong bonds of karma.

“The divine Sekkizhar Peruman, who previously narrated the history of Nambi Arurar becoming Aalala Sundaran and continuing his sacred service, now concludes the epic by showing how the two maidens, the two companions of Ambika, who were born in the South for Aalala Sundaran according to the Lord’s command from Kailash and married Nambi Arurar, returned to their former state and duties. Paravaiyammaiyar became Kamaliniyar, and Sangiliyammaiyar became Aninthithaiyammaiyar. Both became companions of Ambika and continued their former sacred service in the temple of the Daughter of the Mountain Lord. In Thiru-Kailash, they continued their former sacred garden work, such as making garlands for Ambika. They continue to perform sacred services such as offering garlands to the Goddess, and singing and dancing before Her. Kamaliniyar and Aninthithaiyar.

“At the beginning of this epic, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman, who sang in the order of first mentioning the Goddess as ‘Aludaiya Nayaki’, then Aninthithai as second, and Kamalini as third, did so because it was the opening of the epic, placing the Goddess first, followed by Aninthithai and Kamalini.

“Now, at the conclusion of the epic, we see the divine poetic beauty of Sekkizhar Peruman, who, in the order of conclusion, places Kamaliniyar, Aninthithaiyar, and then the Daughter of the Mountain Lord, Ambika, thus concluding in that sequence. The fifty-first Thiru-Padal: “Vazhi Mathavar Aalala Sundarar Vazhiyidai Arul Seitha Ezh.” “Ezh Isai Thiru-Pathigam Ivvulaginil Ettida Eri Munnir Azhi Vendha Nam Varunanukku Aliththida Avanum Avvarul Soodi Oozhiyil Thani Oruvar Tham Thiru-Anjaikkalaththil Oyththu Unarviththan.” The seven-note Thiru-Pathigam, “Thananaimunpadaiyathan,” composed by Aalala Sundarar, the great ascetic who bestows life, on his way to Thiru-Kailash. If we consider that Nambigal, who was Aalala Sundarar, recited this Thiru-Pathigam on his way from Thiru-Anjaikkalam to Thiru-Kailash on the white elephant bestowed by the Lord, then to dispel the doubt of how this Thiru-Pathigam reached this earthly world, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman graciously explains. To spread this Thiru-Pathigam in this world for the salvation of all, Aalala Sundarar gave it to Varunan, the king of the surging oceans, and commanded him. Varunan, bearing that grace upon his head, conveyed that Thiru-Pathigam to Thiru-Anjaikkalam of Lord Shiva, the unique one who is imperishable even through ages, and made it known.

“Thus, that Thiru-Pathigam reached us here and continues to be recited. He also commanded, “Take this and inform Anjaiyappar.” Therefore, Varunan, bearing that command on his head, conveyed it to Thiru-Anjaikkalam. The tenth verse of the Thiru-Pathigam states, “Inform Anjaiyappar of the roaring ocean.” The eightieth verse: “Cherar Kavalar Vinnappam Seitha Aththiru-Ula Puram Andru Saral Velliyang Kaiyilayil Ketta Masaththanar Thariththu Intha Paaril Vediyar Thiru-Pidavoor Thanil Velippada Pakirnthu Engum Narave.”

“Thus, the Thiru-Pathigam composed by our Lord Sundaramurthy Swamigal came to this earth. How did the ‘Thiru-Kailaya Gnana Ula’ Prabandham, submitted by Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, come to this earth? When the doubt arises as to how the ‘Thiru-Ula-Puram’ that Cheraman Perumal Nayanar presented before the Lord in Kailash came to be known on this earth, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman composes this Thiru-Padal to dispel it. On that day, in the Kailash mountain, the ‘Thiru-Kailaya Gnana Ula’ Prabandham, submitted by Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, was heard by Mahasathanar, who is revered as Masaththanar. Mahasathanar, also revered as Aiyanar throughout Tamil Nadu, is known as Harihara Putthirar. The term ‘Sastha’ means one who punishes evil-doers and is skilled in scriptures.

“Such a Mahasasta, having heard that Gnana Ula in Thiru-Kailash, carried it and revealed it in Thiru-Pidavoor, a sacred place on this earth where Vedic scholars reside. Thiru-Pidavoor, a sacred place, is one of the Thevara Vaippu Sthalams. It is located a short distance northwest of Siruganur, on the way from Tiruchirappalli to Perambalur, and a short distance east of Thuraiyur. The Sthalam known as Thiru-Pidavoor is currently called Thiru-Pattur. ‘Pidavoor Nadu Pidavoor’ is the name mentioned in inscriptions. It is also known as ‘Moovayiranallur Brahmadeyam’.

“Here, the Mahasasta temple is very famous. The Mahasasta Aiyanar here gives darshan with the Thiru-Ula manuscript in his hand. He is revered and called ‘Arangetriya Aiyanar’. A Sastha temple has also been established in Thiru-Anjaikkalam. In Thiru-Pidavoor (Thiru-Pattur), the Lord’s Moolamurthy is a Swayambhu Lingam. This is the place where Patanjali and Vyagrapada were relieved of their Saturn affliction.

“It is a place where Brahma worshipped by installing twelve Lingams. Brahma’s large form, of great beauty, is in the first circumambulation. Today, this temple is very famous. Many people visit daily to worship ‘Brahmapureeswar’, who changes destiny. This place is called Thiru-Padaiyur because Lord Muruga received his weapon here. The Kailasanathar temple has been beautifully renovated with stone, resembling a chariot.

“This is the place where the Thillai Moovayiravar worshipped. Indra, Thirumal, Manduka Muni, and the Saptha Kanniyar have worshipped the Lord here and received blessings.

“The Lord is Brahmapureeswar.

“The Lord is Brahmapureeswar. Kailasanathar, the Goddess Brahmnayaki, Gowriyamai, and the Pulipaaichi Theertham are the special features of Thiru-Pidavoor. In such a Thiru-Pidavoor, Mahasasta, having heard the Thiru-Kailaya Gnana Ula in Thiru-Kailash, came to this Thiru-Pidavoor, holding the manuscript in his hand, and performed its public recitation here. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman thus reveals to us that he made it known to this earthly world.

“Thus, our Lord Sundaramurthy Swamigal and Cheraman Perumal Nayanar went to Thiru-Kailash and composed the Thiru-Pathigam and the Thiru-Kailaya Gnana Ula. They departed from Thiru-Anjaikkalam on the Swathi star in the month of Aadi. For over six hundred years, the region where Thiru-Anjaikkalam is located has been dominated by the Malayalam language, and the Tamil language has been forgotten there.

“Therefore, to further explain the greatness of Nambigal and Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, About ninety years ago, in the year 1935 CE, the great orator of Periya Puranam, Sivakavimani Sambandha Saranalaya Swamigal, and some other devotees made great efforts to ensure that special worships, Thevara recitations, and the recitation of Cheraman Perumal Nayanar’s Arulnur, along with the Aadi Swathi Guru Puja, take place in Thiru-Anjaikkalam. As a result of their efforts and divine grace, from that day onwards, the Guru Puja of our Lord Sundaramurthy Swamigal and Cheraman Perumal Nayanar is celebrated annually on the Thiru-Day of Swathi Nakshatra in the month of Karkadaka (Aadi Swathi) by the Sekkizhar Thiru-Kootaththar. It was also in the year 1935 that the writing of the commentary on this Periya Puranam was started by Sivakavimani Sambandha Saranalaya Swamigal. Sivakavimani Aiyya writes that the successful completion of the extensive commentary on Periya Puranam was also the result of the continuous worship performed by Apparai of Anjaikkalam.

“The King of Kochi graciously granted permission for this festival and annually sanctioned one hundred rupees from his government for it. The head of Thiruppanandal Sri Kasi Madam, Srimath Arunandi Thambiran Swamigal, has established a capital of fifty thousand rupees for the Thevara prize on Aadi Swathi day in the name of Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, and seven thousand rupees for the Maheswara Puja during the Aadi Swathi festival, ensuring the continuation of these good deeds. The Kochi royal family has also granted eleven cents of land near the temple in Thiru-Anjaikkalam to the Kovai Sekkizhar Thiru-Kootaththar for the establishment of a choultry in the name of Cheraman. The said choultry was established with five thousand rupees donated by the said heads of Sri Kasi Madam. Sivakavimani Aiyya has noted that Sir Shanmugam Chettiar provided great support for all these.

“Thus, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman sang the sacred history of how our Lord Sundaramurthy Swamigal and Cheraman Perumal Nayanar went from Thiru-Anjaikkalam to Thiru-Kailash, and how the Thiru-Pathigam and Thiru-Kailaya Gnana Ula composed by them reached this earthly world. Aalala Sundarar, as a Shiva Gana Natha, offered garlands to the Lord and performed the sacred service of carrying the sacred ash casket. Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, as a Shiva Gana Natha, was seated there. Kamaliniyar and Aninthithaiyar, as companions of Ambika, served Ambika in Thiru-Kailash. The fifty-third Thiru-Padal of the Vellanaich Charukkam, the Thirteenth Charukkam of the Periya Puranam composed by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman, is the four thousand two hundred and eighty-first Thiru-Padal of the Periya Puranam and the concluding Thiru-Padal. The four thousand two hundred and eighty-first Thiru-Padal and the concluding Thiru-Padal of the Periya Puranam: “Endrum Inbam Perugum Iyalbinal Ondru Kathaliththu Ullamum Ongida Mandrullar Adiyar Avar Van Pugazh Nindrathu Engum Nilavi Ulakalam.” Its meaning is: “Due to the nature of ever-increasing bliss of Lord Shiva, all beings, loving the one beloved Lord, rise under the feet of Shiva.

“The great glories of the Lord of the Dance and His devotees thus stand eternally pervading all worlds.” The Chola King, Anabaya Chola Peruman, asked the divine Sekkizhar Peruman what Shiva’s story could provide certainty in this world and the next. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman, taking the answers given as the core meaning, concludes with a mangalam (auspicious verse) to introduce the great epic, explaining the special and general characteristics of Thiruchitrambalavan, the prime mover of the Saiva Siddhanta path, and the methods of worshipping Him.

“Here, as the author Patanjali stated at the beginning of the Mahabhashya, “Aadhiyum Anthamum Naduvum Mangalam Othiya Mozhigalal Uraippar Ayidin Ethamil Irunila Kizhatthi Enba Neethiyam Panuvalgal Nilavum Enbave” (If one speaks of the beginning, middle, and end with auspicious words, it is said that the flawless earth goddess will be pleased, and righteous scriptures will prevail), the divine Sekkizhar Peruman, who spoke of mangalam at the beginning and in the middle of this Periya Puranam, also speaks of mangalam at the end of the book, having concluded the history of the epic’s hero. Through this, he reiterates the inner meaning stated at the beginning of the book, by way of ‘Mudinthathu Mudiththal’ (concluding what has been concluded). And still, Due to the nature of ever-increasing bliss, with the means that cause bliss to grow continuously, loving the one, the singular Shiva Param-Porul (Supreme Being), with the heart and soul rising, becoming upright and perfect, the glorious history of the devotees of Chitrambalavan, ‘Mandrullar Adiyar Avar Van Pugazh’, stood pervading all worlds, becoming manifest everywhere and established everywhere. The bliss that is not limited by time is therefore ever-increasing bliss, as the divine Sekkizhar Peruman states, “Endrum Inbam Perugum.” The means such as Chariyai and Kiriyai, which have the nature of increasing bliss one after another, are therefore called “Inbam Perugum Iyalbinal.” ‘Ondru’ refers to the Shiva Param-Porul, and ‘Ullam’ refers to the soul. Although there are many souls, it is referred to in the singular as ‘Uyir’ (soul). That is the unity of species.

“The soul, whose knowledge, desire, and actions (Ichcha, Kriya, Gnana) are obscured by Anava Malam, and which is said to be non-existent, then, in the Sakala state, the soul that was trapped in seven births, desiring, with its desire expanding, and as a result, its knowledge and action expanding, the three of the soul—desire, knowledge, and action—stand focused only on the one true being, Lord Shiva. That is the state of rising. In that state of rising, the glory of Lord Shiva, who is in the assembly, and the glory of Shiva’s devotees, who are the devotees of the one in the assembly, the glory of both the Lord and the devotees, is the glory that adds meaning.

“Therefore, that great glory, “Nindrathu Engum Nilavi Ulakalam,” the divine Sekkizhar Peruman’s concluding commentary on the book he undertook, the mangalam, states, “Let it stand and pervade all worlds.” Therefore, “Endrum Inbam Perugum Iyalbinal” (by the nature of ever-increasing bliss), through the three means such as Chariyai, the souls, as the heart, loving the one Shiva, and by loving Shiva, they rise. As stated in Shiva Gnana Bodham, “Kathaliththu Nindrida, Aekamai Nindre Inaiyadikal Ondrurai” (by loving and standing, being one with the twin feet), as they stand in the path of attaining that knowledge, thus, for the souls to attain perfection, to attain the state of Shiva, the glory of the Lord and the glory of the devotees stood pervading all worlds. The mantra, “Ulakalam,” given by Lord Koothaperuman, which the divine Sekkizhar Peruman mentioned at the beginning and in the middle of this Puranam, he concludes with “Ulakalam” at the end. By stating “Ulakalam” in the first verse, He concludes by signifying the culmination of the means. This book, which began with “Ulakelam Unarnthu Otharku Ariyavan” (He who is difficult to know and praise by all worlds), thus concludes with “Engum Nilavi Ulakalam” (pervading all worlds) in a concise manner. We saw in the verse beginning with “Jothi Muththin” that the divine Sekkizhar Peruman placed “Ulakalam” as the central gem of this book when Thiru-Gnanasambandha Peruman ascended the Jothi Muththu Sivikai.

“Therefore, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman has used the auspicious word “Ulakelam” at the beginning, middle, and end.

“Thus, for the continuous growth of bliss, with the means that cause it, all souls, desiring the one Shiva Param-Porul, are freed from their faults and attain perfection, becoming Shiva-like. The glorious history of the devotees of Thiruchitrambalavan, as told in this Puranam, is thus elucidated. The glorious history of the devotees of Thiruchitrambalavan, as told in this Puranam, is thus elucidated and established everywhere in the world. The Thirteenth Charukkam of the Periya Puranam, composed by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman, the Vellanaich Charukkam, is complete. The Thiru-Thondar Puranam, also known as Periya Puranam, composed by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman, is complete. Praise, praise be to the golden feet of Srimath Nataraja Peruman, accompanied by Thillai Sivakami Sundari Amba, the embodiment of all-powerful compassion, who is half of Uma.

“Praise, praise be to the golden feet of the sixty-three Nayanmars and the Thogaiyadiyars. Praise, praise be to the golden feet of our Lord Sundaramurthy Swamigal. Praise, praise be to the feet of the Samayacharyas. Praise, praise be to the feet of Nambiandar Nambigal Peruman. Praise, praise be to the feet of the divine Sekkizhar Peruman. Praise, praise be to the golden feet of Sivakavimani Sambandha Saranalaya Swamigal, the great orator of Periya Puranam.

“Endrum inbam perugum iyalbinaal Ondru kaathaliththu ullamum Ongida Mandrulaar adiyaar avar vaan pugazh Nindradhu engum nilavi ulagalaam Thiruchitrambalam By the nature of ever-increasing bliss, with hearts uplifted in singular devotion, the celestial glory of the devotees of the Lord of the sacred assembly stands pervasive throughout the world.”
Thiruchitrambalam.
